- Code Mode's `image()` takes an array of image items, and the exec description says how to show an image file (2026-11-10, Georgios on the nanocode.xyz demos, where a Python chart saved as a PNG never reached the chat: "it should be able to plot direct things from python ... with the image tool in code mode no?"). Claude's `Read` returns an image file as an array of `{ image_url, detail }` items and `image()` refused arrays, so Haiku 5.5 base64-encoded the PNG through `Bash` instead, whose nested output stops at `NESTED_STREAM_BYTES` (128 KiB): the data URL ended early and the image became `image content omitted because it could not be processed`. `image(array)` now appends each item, after checking them all, and refuses an empty array. When the cell's tools include `Read` or `view_image`, the description adds a Nanocode extension line: `image(await tools.Read({ file_path }))` (or `tools.view_image({ path })`) shows an image file, and a shell's base64 is cut off. Checked on 2026-11-10 with nanocode-ts's module built on this tree (its `output/image-check.ts`, a scripted model and the nanocode-ts shell): `image(await tools.Read(...))` of an 1800×1200 PNG reached the next Claude request as an image block, `image(await tools.view_image(...))` reached Codex as `input_image`, each request's exec description carried its harness's line, and the `Bash` base64 path still came back 131,072 characters long with `truncated: true` and the placeholder. `cargo check --locked --lib -p nanocode-code-mode`, also for `wasm32-unknown-unknown`, passed.
- A `Tools` set with `ToolExposure::DirectOnly` now reaches the model as plain function tools, without Code Mode's `exec` and `wait` (2026-11-11). The Codex and Claude backends set `CodeModeOnly` on every tool set they built, which overwrote a caller's exposure, and `model_specs` always put `exec` and `wait` first. The backends now keep the exposure the caller chose (the default is still `CodeModeOnly`), and `DirectOnly` drops `exec` and `wait`. `crates/nanocode/examples/nc_lite.rs` is nanocode cut down to what fx ships, for the CLI benchmark: one harness (Codex), any model through the AI Gateway, plain function tools, a JSONL session store, `exec`, `--continue` and `sessions`, and no TUI, MCP, computer use or runners. Measured against bench/cli's fixture on one machine, five interleaved runs each (2026-11-11): its first request is 36.9 KB instead of 45.3 KB (12 function tools, 15.0 KB, instead of a 20 KB `exec` and `wait`, against fx's 30 KB request with 16 tools), the request at step 50 of a shell turn is 55.0 KB instead of 67.0, and wall time for 10 and 50 steps did not change beyond noise (median 148 against 159 ms, and 672 against 686). The release `nanocode` CLI still advertises only `exec` and `wait`. `cargo check --locked --lib` of nanocode-tool-host, nanocode-backend-codex and nanocode-backend-claude passed for native and `wasm32-unknown-unknown`.
- A JSONL home keeps each record at least one filesystem block long once, in `records/<blake3>`, and a session file holds a reference to it (2026-11-11). Every session wrote its own copy of the system prompt and tool catalog, the same 33 KB for nc-lite and 53 KB for nanocode in each session, while a resumed session rebuilds its prompt from the running binary's configuration anyway (a nc-lite session resumed with `open_session` before this change sent Codex's 18 KB prompt rather than the one it was created with). fx stores neither; this keeps exact replay and pays for them once per home. `shared.rs` writes a new record to a temporary file, syncs it and renames it without replacing (an existing name is the same content), then syncs the directory, so the reference is never durable before the record. A value below the block size stays inline, and one that starts with the marker (U+0002) is escaped by doubling it; reading checks the content against its name. When a session written before this change commits a record it already holds, the journal compares the decoded values, so old sessions resume (checked: a session written by 9f373a4b9 resumed twice by the new build). Measured on the bench fixture (2026-11-11): a one-turn nc-lite session is 8.9 KB instead of 42.7 KB of file (13.6 KB instead of 52 KB by `du -k`), and a nanocode one is 14.0 KB instead of 68.1 KB, with 37 KB and 53 KB of shared records once per home; 50 saved nc-lite sessions took 18 ms each. What remains per session is nanocode's own state: the environment context stored in both the head record and the history, the permissions text, usage, and the payload written again on every commit. An older build cannot read a record moved to `records/`. `cargo check --locked --lib -p nanocode-store-jsonl`, also for `wasm32-unknown-unknown`, passed.
- nc-lite is its own crate, `bin/nc-lite`, built from the SDK with what fx ships and no more (2026-11-11): one harness (Codex), any model through the AI Gateway, plain function tools, a JSONL home, `exec`, `--continue`, `sessions`, and an inline terminal interface. Its system prompt is fx's (`prompt.md`, adapted under Apache-2.0 as `NOTICE` says: the name, and the lines about skills, context-update blocks and fx's own docs, which nc-lite lacks, are removed), set on the runtime builder so a resumed session gets it too. Its first request is 23.9 KB instead of 36.9 KB with Codex's prompt, against fx's 26 to 30 KB; 15 KB of it is the tool catalog. The interface (`tui.rs`, `markdown.rs`) draws its header before building the runtime, streams each delta as it arrives and restyles a line as Markdown once it is complete, and reads keys on an owning thread that forwards them to one select loop. On the Hand against the fixture, preliminary: first frame 8 to 19 ms against fx's 10 to 13, idle CPU 0.63 ms/s against 6.86, key echo p50 0.21 ms against 1.1, RSS after the first frame 15 MB against 8.8, and the screen 4.7 to 5.3 ms behind the server's last token at 50,000 tokens/s. Its dist binary is 26.9 MB against fx's 12.8.
- A session a CLI is done with is archived: compacted, then stored as `<id>.jsonl.zst`, one format byte and a zstd frame (level 1) against a dictionary built into the binary (`session.dict`, 41.6 KB: compacted nanocode and nc-lite sessions), and the summaries cache is `.summaries.json.zst` (2026-11-11). After shared records (above), a one-turn session was still 7 to 9 KB, mostly template text (permissions, environment context, field names), revisions the journal no longer needs, and hex hashes; the dictionary carries the template, compaction drops the revisions, and zstd packs the rest. `JsonlStore::archiver` returns a handle whose `archive()` compacts and archives every session this process wrote; nc-lite and nanocode call it after `Runtime::shutdown`, after listing once so the summaries cache holds the session, and the cache entry follows the file (compaction's rename, then the archive), so listing never opens an archive. Opening an archived session restores `<id>.jsonl` first (decompress to a temporary file, rename without replacing, remove the archive), so the journal's format and its multi-process rules are unchanged while a session is in use; a session restored only to be read is archived again. Archiving checks that the file did not change while it was read, but a second process writing the same session at that moment can fail with a missing file instead of a fence (eight concurrent `--continue` runs on one session: six fenced, as before, and two missing-file errors); concurrent writers to one session were already refused. `session.dict` belongs to format 1 and must not change; a new dictionary needs a new format byte. Measured on the bench fixture (2026-11-11): 100 one-turn nc-lite sessions take 837 bytes each in `sessions/` (archives and summaries) plus 19.8 KB of shared records per home, against 8.9 KB before this change and 42.7 KB before shared records; nanocode's take 661 bytes plus 52.8 KB (68 KB at the start of the day); a 10-step tool turn is 1.6 KB; fx keeps 2.6 KB per one-turn session. Each archive still occupies a filesystem block, so `du -k` counts 4 KB a session. A save took 35 ms instead of 18 on a loaded Hand with slow fsync (compaction, two summary writes and the archive add four fsyncs). Old homes (plain sessions, `.summaries.json`) open and migrate. `cargo check --locked --lib -p nanocode-store-jsonl`, also for `wasm32-unknown-unknown`, passed.
